# Cylinder amplitudes and logarithmic bridge-length windows on the honeycomb lattice

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0003-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0479` | `PM-237.6` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Cylinder-amplitudes-and-logarithmic-bridge-length-windows-on-the-honeycomb-lattice-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Cylinder-amplitudes-and-logarithmic-bridge-length-windows-on-the-honeycomb-lattice-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). {
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove a logarithmic window of critical honeycomb bridge lengths: summed through height $`2h(\\log h)^{1/64}`$, bridges of lengths between $`h^{4/3}(\\log h)^{-1/8}`$ and $`2h^{4/3}(\\log h)^{1/2}`$ have mass at least $`h^{3/4}(\\log h)^{-C}`$.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a logarithmic window of critical honeycomb bridge lengths: summed through height $`2h(\log h)^{1/64}`$, bridges of lengths between $`h^{4/3}(\log h)^{-1/8}`$ and $`2h^{4/3}(\log h)^{1/2}`$ have mass at least $`h^{3/4}(\log h)^{-C}`$.

- **claim-logarithmic-bridge-window** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-thm-window)): There are constants $c,C>0$ and $h_0$ such that, for every real $h\ge h_0$, with $$R_h=h(\log h)^{1/64},\qquad \ell_-(h)=h^{4/3}(\log h)^{-1/8},\qquad \ell_+(h)=h^{4/3}(\log h)^{1/2},$$ we have $$\sum_{1\le H\le 2R_h} \sum_{\substack{\omega\in\mathcal B_H\\ \ell_-(h)\le\|\omega\|\le2\ell_+(h)}}\mu(\omega) \ge c h^{3/4}(\log h)^{-C}.$$ The sum over $H$ is over integer lattice heights.
  - Explanation: A substantial critical bridge mass lies in the stated logarithmic length window after summing over integer heights. The result preserves height averaging and leaves the lateral endpoint free.
- **claim-finite-boundary-and-polygon-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-thm-finite)): There are positive constants, independent of the integer parameters, such that $$B_H\asymp H^{-1/4},\qquad A_g\asymp g^{-5/4}\quad(H,g\ge1).$$ The lower bound for $A_g$ can be restricted to arches of diameter at most $C g$, for a fixed sufficiently large $C$. For every sufficiently large fixed $T$, bridges of height $H$ whose transverse displacement from the starting normal stays at most $TH$ have mass at least $c_T H^{-1/4}$ and length-weighted mass at most $C_T H^{13/12}$. Either weak sign of terminal transverse displacement may be specified in the mass lower bound. For arches and bridges from a fixed wall port in a strip of height $H$, the total mass of paths with diameter exceeding $r\ge C H$ is at most $$C H^{-1/4}e^{-c r/H}.$$ Finally, for all sufficiently large $R$, $$\sum_{R\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}P<2R}\mu(P)\|P\|^2 \le C R^{2/3}(1+\log\log R).$$
  - Explanation: The finite estimates control bridge and arch masses, confined bridge length mass, strip diameter tails, and polygon length second moments. Confinement parameters are fixed before the size varies.
- **claim-finite-periodic-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-prop-periodic)): For the above periodic model, the marked-loop mass, winding-loop mass modulo row translations, and color sum satisfy $$\begin{aligned} E_+(Z;t)&=1+\kappa\left(\frac{Q(Z,t)Q(Z,q^3t)} {Q(Z)^2\prod_{z\in Z}d(z,t)}-1\right),\label{L:eq:one-port}\\ Y(Z)J(Z;t)&=\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{\sqrt2} [p_Z^+(t)p_Z^-(q^3t)+p_Z^-(t)p_Z^+(q^3t)],\label{L:eq:winding}\\ O_Z(1)&=1,\qquad O_Z(\kappa)=Y(Z),\qquad O_Z(\kappa^2)=Y(Z)^2.\label{L:eq:color-values} \end{aligned}$$ These identities hold initially at physical parameters and their convergence neighborhoods, and elsewhere by rational continuation. Replacing $q$ by $q^{-1}$ in the color factors has the same effect as exchanging $+$ and $-$, including $b_+,b_-$.
  - Explanation: Exact rational identities express marked-loop mass, winding-loop mass, and distinguished color sums through the periodic vacuum polynomials. Their probabilistic interpretation initially uses physical parameters and convergence neighborhoods.
- **claim-half-cylinder-polynomial-vacua** (construction, [source](#stmt-l-prop-vacuum-polynomials)): For each pairing pattern $p$ and seam sign $\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}$, the normalized physical vacuum has the form $$(V^\sigma_N)_p(Z;t) = \frac{\prod_j z_j^{o_j/2}}{A_\delta(Z)}\,\psi_{p}(Z), \qquad \deg_{z_j}\psi_p\le\delta(N-1)-o_j , \qquad \delta=\begin{cases}1&\sigma=1,\\2&\sigma=-1,\end{cases}$$ independent of the row parameter. Here $o_j=1$ for an occupied port of pattern $p$, 0 otherwise. At adjacent high-then-low pairs of rapidities $q^a l,l$ the vector is $P V^\sigma_{N-1}$ (fused rapidity $q l$) for $a=2$ and $C V^\sigma_{N-2}$ for $a=3$, if the seam is not between the two. Branches at a specialization use $(q^a l)^{1/2}=q^{a/2}l^{1/2}$; thus this prescribes values of the branch-independent polynomials $\psi$. The same parity convention holds under all transports. At a variable 0 or infinity the normalized vector $V$ with that port vacant goes to the vector with position dropped, and occupied components vanish there. There is covariance under nearest rapidity exchanges (by $B$, away from the seam) and cyclic rotations (moving the seam with the conjugating sign above), thus also rules across the seam by first rotating.
  - Explanation: Normalized half-cylinder vacua have polynomial numerators with occupation-dependent coordinate degree bounds. Pair fusion, cap removal, extreme-variable limits, and seam covariance characterize the representation.
- **claim-scalar-change-of-channel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-prop-scalar-channel)): Fix $L>0$, ordered angular layers with the prescribed common-layer boundary values, and a finite ordered list of fields with charges $\beta_i$ as above. Use the indicated lifts of $O$ and $M_b$ and the cocycle convention of the fields $V_{\beta_i}$. For $U=M_b$, impose the neutrality condition $\sum_i h\beta_i=0$; without it the scalar trace contribution is zero. Let $\mathcal S_i$ be the displayed self multiplier, $\mathcal C_{ij}$ the exponential of the displayed short-range cross logarithm for $x_i<x_j$, and $\mathcal K_f$ the displayed kinetic factor. The angular trace contribution for this list is $$\label{L:eq:scalar-channel} \mathcal T_U(\boldsymbol\beta,\boldsymbol u) =Z_U a_U\sum_{f\in\mathcal F_U} e^{\pi i\Phi}\mathcal K_f \prod_i\mathcal S_i\prod_{x_i<x_j}\mathcal C_{ij}, \qquad a_{M_b}=\sqrt{\frac{L}{2\pi H_b}},\quad a_O=1.$$ Here $\mathcal F_{M_b}=-\phi/(2\pi)+\mathbb Z$. For $O$ the sum consists of one term, $\mathcal K_f=1$, and all fixed-axis terms in $\Phi$ are omitted. The identity first holds with the separated representatives and prescribed boundary values above; Section <a href="#L:step6" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="L:step6">3.4</a> supplies the convergence and continuation required for the full root expansion.
  - Explanation: The scalar trace separates into oscillator, self-interaction, cross-interaction, kinetic, and fixed-axis summation factors. Neutrality is essential in the transformed channel.
- **claim-long-circle-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-long-circle)): Fix $\phi\in\{-7\pi/8,-\pi/8\}$. Let $S_{\rm raw}(N,\phi)$ denote the confluent raw sum just considered. With $$E(y)=y^2/(2H_b),\quad f_0=-\phi/(2\pi),\quad {\cal R}=d_{M_b}(a)/d_O(a)=(2k/3)^2/\sqrt{2k}$$ (the last by the row formulas), define $$F_N(\phi)= \frac{S_{\rm raw}(N,\phi)} {\|w\|^N{\cal R}^{N^2}N^{7/16-12E(f_0)}}.$$ There is a locally finite set $\Gamma_\phi\subset\mathbb Q_{\ge0}$, containing $0$, and fixed polynomials $P_{\phi,\gamma}$ such that, for every desired accuracy $J>0$, one can choose $A_J>J$ with $$F_N(\phi)= \sum_{\substack{\gamma\in\Gamma_\phi\\ \gamma\le A_J}} N^{-\gamma}P_{\phi,\gamma}(\log N)+O_{\phi,J}(N^{-J})$$ for every sufficiently large integer $N$. The coefficient polynomials are the same for all truncations and all integers $N$; no subsequence or parity choice is made. The polynomial at $\gamma=0$ is a constant, which may at this stage vanish. For $\phi=-\pi/8$, every positive rate is at least $1$.
  - Explanation: The normalized confluent color sum has compatible rational-power expansions with logarithmic polynomial coefficients across all sufficiently large integer sizes. This statement alone permits the leading constant to vanish.
- **claim-positive-homogeneous-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-positive-normalization)): There are constants $c_Q,c_R>0$ such that $$Q_n\sim c_Q B_Q(n)n^{5/48},\qquad R_n\sim c_R B_R(n)n^{-7/24}.$$ After these leading factors are removed, both sequences have expansions in terms $n^{-\gamma}$ with rational $\gamma\ge0$ and polynomial coefficients in $\log n$, to arbitrary inverse-power accuracy. The positive constant leading terms and all coefficient polynomials are common to all integer sizes. Any fixed number of finite differences may be taken by first choosing the expansion to sufficiently high accuracy.
  - Explanation: Positive polynomial norms establish nonzero leading amplitudes for the homogeneous vacuum polynomials. Their compatible expansions support any fixed number of finite differences after increasing the expansion accuracy.
- **claim-polynomial-laplace-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-lem-polynomial-laplace)): The positive Jacobi off-diagonals for $w_b$ satisfy $$\sqrt{h_n/h_{n-1}}= n(2c)^{-1} s_n,\qquad s_n^2=1+\frac{d_0}{n^2}+O(n^{-2-\epsilon})$$ for some real $d_0$ and some $\epsilon>0$; the on-diagonals vanish by symmetry. Put $S_n=\prod_{j=1}^n s_j$; then $h_0 S_\infty^2=k/(2c\sqrt2)$. For $0<\tau<\pi/2$, the ratio below is $1+O(n^{-1})$, locally uniformly in $\tau$, and has the pointwise asymptotic $$\label{L:eq:laplace-correction} \frac{f_n(2c\tau)}{\sqrt{h_0}\, S_n \sec\tau\tan^n\tau} =1+\frac{d(\tau)}{n}+o(n^{-1}).$$
  - Explanation: The even Fourier weight has controlled Jacobi coefficients and a first correction to its polynomial Laplace asymptotic. The correction matters because the leading winding expression cancels after taking real parts.
- **claim-exponential-cylinder-height-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-cylinder-height-tail)): There are positive constants $c_1,c_2,C$ and $n_0$ such that, for every regular honeycomb cylinder with $n\ge n_0$ columns and every $u\ge0$, $$\label{L:eq:cylinder-height-tail} \sum_{P\ {\rm winding}} X^{\|P\|}e^{c_1h(P)/n}\le C/n, \qquad \sum_{\substack{P\ {\rm contractible}\\h(P)>u}}X^{\|P\|} \le C n^C e^{-c_2u/n}.$$
  - Explanation: Winding polygons have a uniform exponential height moment at the circumference scale. Contractible polygons satisfy an exponential height tail with a polynomial circumference factor.
- **claim-uniform-pressure-root-continuation** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-lem-pressure-root-continuation)): For all sufficiently large $n$, the homogeneous roots at $d=i$ satisfy $\min_jB_j\ge b_*>0$ and $$\\|\mathcal L_n^{-1}v\\|_B\le C\\|v\\|_\infty.$$ There is a fixed disk about $a=0$ on which they continue analytically as simple roots, with $\|\theta_j(a)-\theta_j(0)\|\le C\|a\|/B_j$.
  - Explanation: A weighted inverse estimate controls both bulk and sparse end roots uniformly in circumference. It yields analytic continuation on a fixed parameter disk with root displacement scaled by local density.
- **claim-pressure-discrepancy-quadrature** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-lem-pressure-quadrature)): For the real root deformations just constructed, let $p$ be a smooth test whose value and first two derivatives are bounded by $D\exp(-4\|x-b\|/3)$. Uniformly over the fixed real phase interval, $$\left\|\int p r\right\|\le C D e^{4\|b\|/3}/n .$$
  - Explanation: Smooth exponentially localized tests exhibit cancellation against the root discrepancy uniformly over the constructed real phase interval.
- **claim-open-strip-crossing-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-strip-mass)): There are constants $0<c<C<\infty$ such that, for every integer $H\ge1$, $$cH^{-1/4}\le B_H\le CH^{-1/4}.$$ The bound holds in each of the three lattice-side directions.
  - Explanation: Strict bridge crossing mass obeys matching power bounds at every integer strip height in each lattice-side direction.
- **claim-half-strip-polynomial-vectors** (construction, [source](#stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials)): For either vector $V=E,O$, with $\sigma=-1,+1$ respectively, let $o_j$ denote the occupations of the output pattern. Then $$\begin{gathered} D_n=Q_o\prod_j z_j^{n-1},\qquad \psi= D_n \left(\prod_j z_j^{\sigma o_j/2}\right)V,\\ \psi\text{ is polynomial with }\deg_{z_j}\psi\le m_j=2(n-1)+\sigma o_j. \end{gathered}$$ At ordered neighboring high-low parameters $q^r l,l$ for $r=2,3$, the vector value uses respectively $P V$ (at fused parameter $q l$) and $C V$ (dropping the pair). There are also direct wall rules:* - *At a first parameter $p^{-1}$, insert a vacancy on either smaller vector, and in the odd vector also insert a defect there from the smaller even vector, summing; call this $K_L(E,O)$.* - *At a last parameter $p$, just insert a vacancy on each ($K_R$).* *Even vectors drop a vacant site at $z=0,\infty$ (occupied components go to zero). Odd vectors also satisfy the following edge refinements at the first or last site $e$. With it occupied and paired, $V$ has vanishing leading term both at $z_e=0,\infty$ on the scale $z_e^{\mp1/2}$, respectively. With it the defect, $$\lim_{z_e\to\infty} V/\sqrt{z_e}=c_* E_{\rm dropped},\qquad c_*=[z^{1/2}]g_z$$ where the bracket on the right denotes a Laurent coefficient. All parameter rules concern generic other values.
  - Explanation: Open-strip even and defect vectors admit polynomial representations with pair reductions and direct wall insertion rules. Occupied paired edges and edge defects have distinct limiting behavior.
- **claim-doubled-root-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-lem-doubled-stability)): For the ten root species and angular layers in <a href="#L:eq3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="L:eq3">[L:eq3]</a>, one can choose $d_f<1$ sufficiently close to $1$ and constants $0<\eta<1$ and $c,C>0$ so that the following holds. If the total root $h$-charge is zero, $K$ roots lie on a real circle of sufficiently large period $L$, and $K_I$ counts them in successive bins of lengths between $1/2$ and $2$, then, for every allowed axis index $f$, $$\|\mathscr W_{\rm root}\|\, \exp\!\left[-(1-\eta)\sum_{\rm gaps} \Delta x\,E\Bigl(f+\sum_{\rm before}hr_i\Bigr)\right] \le \exp\!\left(CK-c\sum_I K_I^2\right), \qquad E(y)=\frac{y^2}{2H}.$$ The constants are uniform for the common small-slope bends used in the change of channel, with real gap lengths in the kinetic modulus. They are locally uniform at bounded positive real periods as well, with bin lengths bounded above and below by positive constants chosen for the compact period interval.
  - Explanation: The doubled root system becomes stable after borrowing part of the kinetic propagation. The estimate preserves a negative bin-square penalty and remains valid under the prescribed small-slope bends.
- **claim-two-port-color-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity)): $$Q(a,\mathcal X,b,\mathcal Y)^2 C_{ab} = \rho a b \prod_{i<j}D_{\sigma_i\sigma_j} \sum_{\sum \sigma_i t_{c_i}=0} \prod_{i<j}P^{\sigma_i\sigma_j}_{c_i c_j}(z_j/z_i) \prod_i w_{c_i}^{\sigma_i} f_{c_i}(a,z_i) f_{c_i}(b,z_i)\label{L:eq7}$$ where $f_c(p,z)=(p-q^r z)(p-q^{r'}z)$ for $(r,r')=(5,6),(3,5),(2,3)$ respectively.
  - Explanation: The mass of a single polygon through the named slice ports is represented by a neutral divided-slice color sum with mark polynomials.
- **claim-uniform-two-scale-expansion** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-two-scale)): For an admissible integer array of either of the two kinds specified above, put $$G_Z^{(p_*)}=\frac{F_Z^{(p_*)}}{{\cal D}(Z)^2(K_0s_1)^{3/8}}.$$ For each $J>0$ and each of the fixed composition neighborhoods, there is a threshold $s_J$ independent of $K_0\ge1$ such that, on every fixed phase class and every admissible integer array with $s_1\ge s_J$, this quantity has a finite approximation $$G_Z^{(p_*)}= \sum_\gamma s_1^{-\gamma}P_\gamma(\log s_1;K_0,\text{parameters}) +O_J(K_0^{C_2}s_1^{-J}).\label{L:eq9}$$ The formula is asserted at admissible integer arrays. Its coefficient functions extend holomorphically to the composition neighborhoods above and are bounded by $C_JK_0^{C_2}$. One exponent $C_2$ works for every accuracy $J$; the finite rate and degree lists may depend on $J$ but not on $K_0$. In the marked case $\gamma\ge3/2$, with no logarithm at equality. In the unmarked case $\gamma\ge1/6$, with no logarithm at equality, and all other rates are at least $1/2$ higher.
  - Explanation: Marked and unmarked observables have asymptotic expansions uniform in the scale ratio, with a remainder exponent independent of requested accuracy. Phase classes and admissible integer compositions remain part of the regime.
- **claim-finite-chord-length-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-box-moment)): For every finite convex domain of triangles with a bounded number of lattice sides and diameter at most $C_0r$, the critical length-weighted mass of all boundary-to-boundary chords, with both endpoints summed, is at most $C r^{25/12}$. In particular, for a fixed $T$, the length-weighted mass of strict bridges of height $d$ from one fixed source and transverse displacement bounded by $Td$ is at most $C_Td^{13/12}$.
  - Explanation: A finite convex-domain chord length bound yields a fixed-source first-length bound for transversely confined bridges by translating sources into a common box.
- **claim-recoverable-length-controlled-growth** (construction, [source](#stmt-l-prop-length-controlled-growth)): Let $n,u$ be signed normal level coordinates in two lattice directions whose unit inward vectors have dot product $1/2$. Let $A$ be a port on the $n=0$ wall, and measure both coordinates relative to $A$. There are constants $r_0,c,C,c'>0$, independent of $r$, such that for every $r\ge r_0$ there is a family of distinct prefixes from $A$ with total critical mass at least $c$, whose terminal $u$-levels $s$ lie in the grid bin $[r,2r]$. Every prefix has all centers in $n>0$ and $u<s$, diameter at most $Cr$, center length at most $Cr^{4/3}$, and terminal $n$-coordinate at least $c'r$. Its terminal cut is selected by the first-cut rule and is recoverable after any extension strictly beyond it in $u$.* *For each fixed sufficiently small $\gamma>0$, there are a threshold $r_\gamma\ge r_0$ and positive constants $K_\gamma,c_\gamma,C_\gamma$, and $C_{1,\gamma}$ such that, for every $r\ge r_\gamma$ and every admissible grid target $S\ge K_\gamma r$, these prefixes can be continued to the exact wall $u=S$, with terminal port free, so that all centers remain in $n>0$ and $u<S$, the diameter is at most $C_\gamma S$, the center length is at most $C_\gamma S^{4/3}$, and the resulting family has total mass at least $$c_\gamma S^{-1/4}(S/r)^{-C_{1,\gamma}}.$$ During the continuation, the transverse distance from the positive $u$-normal ray through the selected prefix endpoint is at most $\gamma u$. The cuts separating all stages are recovered by the same first-cut rule.
  - Explanation: Positive-mass half-plane prefixes have controlled diameter, length, clearance, and recoverable terminal cuts. Staged continuation reaches an admissible exact target wall while preserving separation and recoverability.
- **claim-caps-at-prescribed-gaps** (bound, [source](#stmt-l-prop-caps)): There are constants $c,C>0$ such that, for every integer $g\ge1$, $$\sum_{\substack{\omega\text{ an arch at gap }g\\ \operatorname{diam}\omega\le Cg}} \mu(\omega)\ge c g^{-5/4}.$$ Moreover, the unrestricted arch masses satisfy $A_g\ge c A_q$ whenever $1\le g<q$.
  - Explanation: Arches at every prescribed positive gap retain the boundary power lower bound under a fixed diameter cutoff. A boundary deformation also compares unrestricted masses at smaller and larger gaps.
- **claim-critical-irreducible-bridge-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-l-prop-irreducible)): The sum of $X^{\|\gamma\|}$ over irreducible strict bridges $\gamma$ from a fixed port is one. Under this probability law, if $Y$ and $D$ are the height and diameter of the sampled bridge, then $$1-\mathbb E e^{-tY}\asymp t^{3/4}\quad(0<t\le1),\qquad \Pr(D>r)\le C r^{-3/4}\quad(r\ge1).$$ Independent samples may be concatenated to form an infinite self-avoiding port path.
  - Explanation: Irreducible strict bridge weights normalize to a probability law with height-transform and diameter-tail bounds. Independent pieces concatenate through disjoint slabs into an infinite self-avoiding path.

        "exact_quote": "For each pairing pattern $p$ and seam sign $\\sigma\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$, the normalized physical vacuum has the form $$(V^\\sigma_N)_p(Z;t)\n    = \\frac{\\prod_j z_j^{o_j/2}}{A_\\delta(Z)}\\,\\psi_{p}(Z),\n \\qquad \\deg_{z_j}\\psi_p\\le\\delta(N-1)-o_j ,\n \\qquad\n \\delta=\\begin{cases}1&\\sigma=1,\\\\2&\\sigma=-1,\\end{cases}$$ independent of the row parameter. Branches at a specialization use $(q^a l)^{1/2}=q^{a/2}l^{1/2}$; thus this prescribes values of the branch-independent polynomials $\\psi$. The same parity convention holds under all transports. At a variable 0 or infinity the normalized vector $V$ with that port vacant goes to the vector with position dropped, and occupied components vanish there.         "exact_quote": "Fix $L>0$, ordered angular layers with the prescribed common-layer boundary values, and a finite ordered list of fields with charges $\\beta_i$ as above. Use the indicated lifts of $O$ and $M_b$ and the cocycle convention of the fields $V_{\\beta_i}$. For $U=M_b$, impose the neutrality condition $\\sum_i h\\beta_i=0$; without it the scalar trace contribution is zero. Let $\\mathcal S_i$ be the displayed self multiplier, $\\mathcal C_{ij}$ the exponential of the displayed short-range cross logarithm for $x_i<x_j$, and $\\mathcal K_f$ the displayed kinetic factor. The angular trace contribution for this list is $$\\label{L:eq:scalar-channel}\n \\mathcal T_U(\\boldsymbol\\beta,\\boldsymbol u)\n =Z_U a_U\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F_U}\n   e^{\\pi i\\Phi}\\mathcal K_f\n   \\prod_i\\mathcal S_i\\prod_{x_i<x_j}\\mathcal C_{ij},\n \\qquad\n a_{M_b}=\\sqrt{\\frac{L}{2\\pi H_b}},\\quad a_O=1.$$ Here $\\mathcal F_{M_b}=-\\phi/(2\\pi)+\\mathbb Z$. For $O$ the sum consists of one term, $\\mathcal K_f=1$, and all fixed-axis terms in $\\Phi$ are omitted. The identity first holds with the separated representatives and prescribed boundary values above; Section <a href=\"#L:step6\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"L:step6\">3.4</a> supplies the convergence and continuation required for the full root expansion.",

For $U=M_b$, impose the neutrality condition $\\sum_i h\\beta_i=0$; without it the scalar trace contribution is zero. Let $\\mathcal S_i$ be the displayed self multiplier, $\\mathcal C_{ij}$ the exponential of the displayed short-range cross logarithm for $x_i<x_j$, and $\\mathcal K_f$ the displayed kinetic factor. The angular trace contribution for this list is $$\\label{L:eq:scalar-channel}\n \\mathcal T_U(\\boldsymbol\\beta,\\boldsymbol u)\n =Z_U a_U\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F_U}\n   e^{\\pi i\\Phi}\\mathcal K_f\n   \\prod_i\\mathcal S_i\\prod_{x_i<x_j}\\mathcal C_{ij},\n \\qquad\n a_{M_b}=\\sqrt{\\frac{L}{2\\pi H_b}},\\quad a_O=1.$$ Here $\\mathcal F_{M_b}=-\\phi/(2\\pi)+\\mathbb Z$. For $O$ the sum consists of one term, $\\mathcal K_f=1$, and all fixed-axis terms in $\\Phi$ are omitted. The identity first holds with the separated representatives and prescribed boundary values above; Section <a href=\"#L:step6\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"L:step6\">3.4</a> supplies the convergence and continuation required for the full root expansion.",
      "explanation": "The scalar trace separates into oscillator, self-interaction, cross-interaction, kinetic, and fixed-axis summation factors. Neutrality is essential in the transformed channel.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\phi\\in\\{-7\\pi/8,-\\pi/8\\}$. Let $S_{\\rm raw}(N,\\phi)$ denote the confluent raw sum just considered. With $$E(y)=y^2/(2H_b),\\quad f_0=-\\phi/(2\\pi),\\quad\n {\\cal R}=d_{M_b}(a)/d_O(a)=(2k/3)^2/\\sqrt{2k}$$ (the last by the row formulas), define $$F_N(\\phi)=\n \\frac{S_{\\rm raw}(N,\\phi)}\n {|w|^N{\\cal R}^{N^2}N^{7/16-12E(f_0)}}.$$ There is a locally finite set $\\Gamma_\\phi\\subset\\mathbb Q_{\\ge0}$, containing $0$, and fixed polynomials $P_{\\phi,\\gamma}$ such that, for every desired accuracy $J>0$, one can choose $A_J>J$ with $$F_N(\\phi)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_\\phi\\\\ \\gamma\\le A_J}}\n N^{-\\gamma}P_{\\phi,\\gamma}(\\log N)+O_{\\phi,J}(N^{-J})$$ for every sufficiently large integer $N$. The coefficient polynomials are the same for all truncations and all integers $N$; no subsequence or parity choice is made. The polynomial at $\\gamma=0$ is a constant, which may at this stage vanish. For $\\phi=-\\pi/8$, every positive rate is at least $1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.6"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-positive-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_Q,c_R>0$ such that $$Q_n\\sim c_Q B_Q(n)n^{5/48},\\qquad\n R_n\\sim c_R B_R(n)n^{-7/24}.$$ After these leading factors are removed, both sequences have expansions in terms $n^{-\\gamma}$ with rational $\\gamma\\ge0$ and polynomial coefficients in $\\log n$, to arbitrary inverse-power accuracy.         "anchor": "stmt-l-lem-polynomial-laplace",
        "exact_quote": "The positive Jacobi off-diagonals for $w_b$ satisfy $$\\sqrt{h_n/h_{n-1}}= n(2c)^{-1} s_n,\\qquad s_n^2=1+\\frac{d_0}{n^2}+O(n^{-2-\\epsilon})$$ for some real $d_0$ and some $\\epsilon>0$; the on-diagonals vanish by symmetry. Put $S_n=\\prod_{j=1}^n s_j$; then $h_0 S_\\infty^2=k/(2c\\sqrt2)$. For $0<\\tau<\\pi/2$, the ratio below is $1+O(n^{-1})$, locally uniformly in $\\tau$, and has the pointwise asymptotic $$\\label{L:eq:laplace-correction}\n \\frac{f_n(2c\\tau)}{\\sqrt{h_0}\\, S_n \\sec\\tau\\tan^n\\tau}\n       =1+\\frac{d(\\tau)}{n}+o(n^{-1}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.6"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There are positive constants $c_1,c_2,C$ and $n_0$ such that, for every regular honeycomb cylinder with $n\\ge n_0$ columns and every $u\\ge0$, $$\\label{L:eq:cylinder-height-tail}\n \\sum_{P\\ {\\rm winding}} X^{|P|}e^{c_1h(P)/n}\\le C/n,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{\\substack{P\\ {\\rm contractible}\\\\h(P)>u}}X^{|P|}\n       \\le C n^C e^{-c_2u/n}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials",
        "exact_quote": "For either vector $V=E,O$, with $\\sigma=-1,+1$ respectively, let $o_j$ denote the occupations of the output pattern. Then $$\\begin{gathered}\n D_n=Q_o\\prod_j z_j^{n-1},\\qquad\n \\psi= D_n \\left(\\prod_j z_j^{\\sigma o_j/2}\\right)V,\\\\\n \\psi\\text{ is polynomial with }\\deg_{z_j}\\psi\\le m_j=2(n-1)+\\sigma o_j.\n\\end{gathered}$$ At ordered neighboring high-low parameters $q^r l,l$ for $r=2,3$, the vector value uses respectively $P V$ (at fused parameter $q l$) and $C V$ (dropping the pair). There are also direct wall rules:*\n\n- *At a first parameter $p^{-1}$, insert a vacancy on either smaller vector, and in the odd vector also insert a defect there from the smaller even vector, summing; call this $K_L(E,O)$.*\n\n- *At a last parameter $p$, just insert a vacancy on each ($K_R$).*\n\n*Even vectors drop a vacant site at $z=0,\\infty$ (occupied components go to zero). Odd vectors also satisfy the following edge refinements at the first or last site $e$. With it occupied and paired, $V$ has vanishing leading term both at $z_e=0,\\infty$ on the scale $z_e^{\\mp1/2}$, respectively. With it the defect, $$\\lim_{z_e\\to\\infty} V/\\sqrt{z_e}=c_* E_{\\rm dropped},\\qquad\n c_*=[z^{1/2}]g_z$$ where the bracket on the right denotes a Laurent coefficient. All parameter rules concern generic other values.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials",
          "exact_quote": "For either vector $V=E,O$, with $\\sigma=-1,+1$ respectively, let $o_j$ denote the occupations of the output pattern. Then $$\\begin{gathered}\n D_n=Q_o\\prod_j z_j^{n-1},\\qquad\n \\psi= D_n \\left(\\prod_j z_j^{\\sigma o_j/2}\\right)V,\\\\\n \\psi\\text{ is polynomial with }\\deg_{z_j}\\psi\\le m_j=2(n-1)+\\sigma o_j.\n\\end{gathered}$$ At ordered neighboring high-low parameters $q^r l,l$ for $r=2,3$, the vector value uses respectively $P V$ (at fused parameter $q l$) and $C V$ (dropping the pair). There are also direct wall rules:*\n\n- *At a first parameter $p^{-1}$, insert a vacancy on either smaller vector, and in the odd vector also insert a defect there from the smaller even vector, summing; call this $K_L(E,O)$.*\n\n- *At a last parameter $p$, just insert a vacancy on each ($K_R$).*\n\n*Even vectors drop a vacant site at $z=0,\\infty$ (occupied components go to zero). Odd vectors also satisfy the following edge refinements at the first or last site $e$. With it occupied and paired, $V$ has vanishing leading term both at $z_e=0,\\infty$ on the scale $z_e^{\\mp1/2}$, respectively. With it the defect, $$\\lim_{z_e\\to\\infty} V/\\sqrt{z_e}=c_* E_{\\rm dropped},\\qquad\n c_*=[z^{1/2}]g_z$$ where the bracket on the right denotes a Laurent coefficient. All parameter rules concern generic other values.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-l-lem-doubled-stability",
        "exact_quote": "For the ten root species and angular layers in <a href=\"#L:eq3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"L:eq3\">[L:eq3]</a>, one can choose $d_f<1$ sufficiently close to $1$ and constants $0<\\eta<1$ and $c,C>0$ so that the following holds. If the total root $h$-charge is zero, $K$ roots lie on a real circle of sufficiently large period $L$, and $K_I$ counts them in successive bins of lengths between $1/2$ and $2$, then, for every allowed axis index $f$, $$|\\mathscr W_{\\rm root}|\\,\n \\exp\\!\\left[-(1-\\eta)\\sum_{\\rm gaps}\n       \\Delta x\\,E\\Bigl(f+\\sum_{\\rm before}hr_i\\Bigr)\\right]\n \\le \\exp\\!\\left(CK-c\\sum_I K_I^2\\right),\n \\qquad E(y)=\\frac{y^2}{2H}.$$ The constants are uniform for the common small-slope bends used in the change of channel, with real gap lengths in the kinetic modulus. They are locally uniform at bounded positive real periods as well, with bin lengths bounded above and below by positive constants chosen for the compact period interval.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.6"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity",
        "exact_quote": "$$Q(a,\\mathcal X,b,\\mathcal Y)^2 C_{ab} =\n \\rho a b \\prod_{i<j}D_{\\sigma_i\\sigma_j}\n \\sum_{\\sum \\sigma_i t_{c_i}=0}\n \\prod_{i<j}P^{\\sigma_i\\sigma_j}_{c_i c_j}(z_j/z_i)\n \\prod_i w_{c_i}^{\\sigma_i} f_{c_i}(a,z_i) f_{c_i}(b,z_i)\\label{L:eq7}$$ where $f_c(p,z)=(p-q^r z)(p-q^{r'}z)$ for $(r,r')=(5,6),(3,5),(2,3)$ respectively.",

        "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-two-scale",
        "exact_quote": "For an admissible integer array of either of the two kinds specified above, put $$G_Z^{(p_*)}=\\frac{F_Z^{(p_*)}}{{\\cal D}(Z)^2(K_0s_1)^{3/8}}.$$ For each $J>0$ and each of the fixed composition neighborhoods, there is a threshold $s_J$ independent of $K_0\\ge1$ such that, on every fixed phase class and every admissible integer array with $s_1\\ge s_J$, this quantity has a finite approximation $$G_Z^{(p_*)}=\n \\sum_\\gamma s_1^{-\\gamma}P_\\gamma(\\log s_1;K_0,\\text{parameters})\n       +O_J(K_0^{C_2}s_1^{-J}).\\label{L:eq9}$$ The formula is asserted at admissible integer arrays. Its coefficient functions extend holomorphically to the composition neighborhoods above and are bounded by $C_JK_0^{C_2}$. One exponent $C_2$ works for every accuracy $J$; the finite rate and degree lists may depend on $J$ but not on $K_0$. In the marked case $\\gamma\\ge3/2$, with no logarithm at equality. In the unmarked case $\\gamma\\ge1/6$, with no logarithm at equality, and all other rates are at least $1/2$ higher.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.6"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-box-moment",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite convex domain of triangles with a bounded number of lattice sides and diameter at most $C_0r$, the critical length-weighted mass of all boundary-to-boundary chords, with both endpoints summed, is at most $C r^{25/12}$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-length-controlled-growth",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"L:prop:recoverable-growth\" label=\"L:prop:recoverable-growth\"></span> Let $n,u$ be signed normal level coordinates in two lattice directions whose unit inward vectors have dot product $1/2$. Let $A$ be a port on the $n=0$ wall, and measure both coordinates relative to $A$. There are constants $r_0,c,C,c'>0$, independent of $r$, such that for every $r\\ge r_0$ there is a family of distinct prefixes from $A$ with total critical mass at least $c$, whose terminal $u$-levels $s$ lie in the grid bin $[r,2r]$. Every prefix has all centers in $n>0$ and $u<s$, diameter at most $Cr$, center length at most $Cr^{4/3}$, and terminal $n$-coordinate at least $c'r$. Its terminal cut is selected by the first-cut rule and is recoverable after any extension strictly beyond it in $u$.*\n\n*For each fixed sufficiently small $\\gamma>0$, there are a threshold $r_\\gamma\\ge r_0$ and positive constants $K_\\gamma,c_\\gamma,C_\\gamma$, and $C_{1,\\gamma}$ such that, for every $r\\ge r_\\gamma$ and every admissible grid target $S\\ge K_\\gamma r$, these prefixes can be continued to the exact wall $u=S$, with terminal port free, so that all centers remain in $n>0$ and $u<S$, the diameter is at most $C_\\gamma S$, the center length is at most $C_\\gamma S^{4/3}$, and the resulting family has total mass at least $$c_\\gamma S^{-1/4}(S/r)^{-C_{1,\\gamma}}.$$ During the continuation, the transverse distance from the positive $u$-normal ray through the selected prefix endpoint is at most $\\gamma u$. The cuts separating all stages are recovered by the same first-cut rule.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-length-controlled-growth",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"L:prop:recoverable-growth\" label=\"L:prop:recoverable-growth\"></span> Let $n,u$ be signed normal level coordinates in two lattice directions whose unit inward vectors have dot product $1/2$.       "exact_statement": "<span id=\"L:prop:recoverable-growth\" label=\"L:prop:recoverable-growth\"></span> Let $n,u$ be signed normal level coordinates in two lattice directions whose unit inward vectors have dot product $1/2$. The cuts separating all stages are recovered by the same first-cut rule.",
      "explanation": "Positive-mass half-plane prefixes have controlled diameter, length, clearance, and recoverable terminal cuts. Staged continuation reaches an admissible exact target wall while preserving separation and recoverability.",

        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **port** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *port* is the midpoint of a triangle side.
- **critical port-path weight** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A path between ports visits a self-avoiding sequence of triangle centers and has critical weight $$\mu(\omega)=X^{\|\omega\|},\qquad X=(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2},$$ where $\|\omega\|$ counts the visited centers.
- **strict bridge** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *strict bridge* of height $H\ge1$ starts at a fixed port on line $0$, ends at any port on line $H$, and has all centers strictly between the two lines.
- **bridge crossing mass** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Write $$B_H=\sum_{\omega\in\mathcal B_H}\mu(\omega),\qquad B_0=1.$$
- **bounded-factor comparison** ([source](#pm-h-2)): $$* </div> Here and throughout, $f\asymp g$ means two inequalities with positive constants independent of the size parameter.
- **periodic marked-loop mass** ([source](#L:part1)): Let $E_+(Z;t)$ denote their mass when required to contain the mark.
- **periodic winding-loop mass** ([source](#L:part1)): Let $J(Z;t)$ denote instead the mass of single winding loops modulo translations by whole rows (no mark condition, and quotient by vertical/row translations only, NOT cyclic shifts of columns).
- **periodic vacuum kernels and polynomials** ([source](#L:part1)): Use the following notation: $$\begin{aligned} h(x,y)&=x^2+y^2+\sqrt2 xy,\qquad K(x,y)=(x^2+y^2)h(x,y),\\ d(x,t)&=(x-q^5t)(x-q^6t),\quad F(Z)=\prod_{i<j} h(z_i,z_j)/(z_i-z_j),\\ Q(Z)&=F(Z)\operatorname{Pf}_{\rm ext}[(x^2-y^2)/h(x,y)]_{x,y\in Z},\\ M(x,y)&=kxy/h(x,y),\qquad R(Z)=F(Z)^2\det M(Z,Z),\\ Y(Z)&=\kappa^N Q(Z)^2/R(Z),\\ p_Z^\pm(s)&=s^{\pm1}-M(s,Z) M(Z,Z)^{-1}(z^{\pm1})_{z\in Z}. \end{aligned}$$
- **periodic color sum** ([source](#L:part1)): For the color sum choose constants $b_0=k$, $b_+b_-=k/2$ and $b_++b_-+k=r$, and define $$\begin{aligned} S(u)&=(u+1)^2/[(u-q)(u-q^{-1})],\\ P_{++}&=P_{--}=1,\qquad P_{00}(u)=S(u),\\ P_{+0}(u)&=P_{0-}(u)=S(u/q),\\ P_{+-}(u)&=S(u/q)S(u/q^2),\qquad P_{ba}(u)=P_{ab}(1/u). \end{aligned}$$
- **charge-space bilinear structure** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Use real vectors $D_j$, $j\bmod8$, with one relation $\sum D_j=0$; take the symmetric bilinear product $$D_0 D_j=(2,-1,1,-2,2,-2,1,-1)_j,\qquad O D_j=D_{j+1}$$ translated cyclically.
- **root zero modes** ([source](#pm-h-15)): For a norm-two root $b'$ used below, set $e_{b'}=-[V_{b'}]_0,\ f_{b'}=[V_{-b'}]_0$.
- **root displacement norm** ([source](#pm-h-36)): Its natural displacement norm is $\\|\eta\\|_B=\max_j B_j\|\eta_j\|$: a root in a sparse tail is allowed a larger displacement than one in the bulk.
- **open-strip boundary Pfaffian** ([source](#L:part6)): Use a different Pfaffian here than in the periodic problem: $$H(x,y)=x^2+y^2+\sqrt2 xy-\tfrac12,\quad F_o=\prod_{i<j}\frac{H(x_i,x_j)}{x_i-x_j},\quad Q_o= F_o\operatorname{Pf}_{\rm ext}\!\left[\frac{(x_i-x_j)(x_i+x_j+d)}{H(x_i,x_j)}\right] .$$
- **plane enclosing-loop gas** ([source](#L:step13)): Write $\mathcal Z_R(o)$ for the weight of sets of disjoint honeycomb polygons within distance $R$ of $o$, each strictly enclosing $o$, with fugacity 2, including the empty set.
- **first qualifying cut** ([source](#L:part10)): The *first qualifying cut* is the lowest candidate with this cut property whose prefix satisfies the specified diameter, confinement, terminal-position, and, when present, length conditions.
- **irreducible strict bridge** ([source](#L:step17)): Cutting a strict bridge at all its intermediate single-crossing levels decomposes it uniquely into strict bridges with no intermediate single-crossing level; these are the *irreducible bridges*.
- **simple double cut** ([source](#L:step19)): A simple double cut is a lattice-side grid line meeting a polygon at exactly two crossing ports.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-port-activity | critical port-path activity | = | $(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}$ | The length counts visited centers. |
| q-window-height-scale | height scale | = | $h(\log h)^{1/64}$ | The height sum uses integer lattice heights. |
| q-window-lower-length | lower length scale | = | $h^{4/3}(\log h)^{-1/8}$ | The threshold is applied in the height-averaged bridge window. |
| q-window-upper-length-scale | upper length scale | = | $h^{4/3}(\log h)^{1/2}$ | The theorem uses the stated multiple of this upper scale. |
| q-window-critical-mass | critical bridge mass in the height-averaged length window | ≥ | $c h^{3/4}(\log h)^{-C}$ | Both the height range and length window are those in the theorem. |
| q-strip-crossing-lower-mass | strip crossing mass $B_H$ | ≥ | $cH^{-1/4}$ | Constants are independent of the integer strip height. |
| q-strip-crossing-upper-mass | strip crossing mass $B_H$ | ≤ | $CH^{-1/4}$ | The bound holds in each lattice-side direction. |
| q-prescribed-cap-lower-mass | arch mass at prescribed gap under the stated diameter cutoff | ≥ | $c g^{-5/4}$ | The diameter cutoff is proportional to the prescribed gap. |
| q-confined-bridge-first-length-mass | length-weighted confined bridge mass | ≤ | $C_Td^{13/12}$ | The confinement parameter is fixed and may affect the prefactor. |
| q-convex-domain-chord-first-length-mass | boundary chord length-weighted mass | ≤ | $C r^{25/12}$ | Both chord endpoints are summed. |
| q-polygon-second-length-mass | polygon squared-length mass in the diameter shell | ≤ | $C R^{2/3}(1+\log\log R)$ | The diameter lies in the theorem's stated shell. |
| q-strip-large-diameter-tail | mass of arches and bridges exceeding the diameter threshold | ≤ | $C H^{-1/4}e^{-c r/H}$ | The threshold exceeds the strip height by the stated fixed factor. |
| q-calibrated-winding-mass | winding polygon mass $J_n$ | ~ | $\frac{\sqrt3}{6n}$ | Circumference grows; only whole-row translations are quotiented. |
| q-winding-exponential-height-moment | winding polygon exponential height moment | ≤ | $C/n$ | Height is measured in row units and the exponential tilt is scaled by circumference. |
| q-contractible-cylinder-height-tail | contractible polygon mass beyond height $u$ | ≤ | $C n^C e^{-c_2u/n}$ | The circumference is sufficiently large. |
| q-half-cylinder-coordinate-degree | vacuum numerator coordinate degree | ≤ | $\delta(N-1)-o_j$ | The sector determines the denominator and occupation adjusts the coordinate degree. |
| q-half-strip-coordinate-degree | half-strip numerator coordinate degree | ≤ | $2(n-1)+\sigma o_j$ | The sector sign and output occupation determine the coordinate bound. |
| q-marked-two-scale-minimal-rate | marked expansion rate $\gamma$ | ≥ | $3/2$ | There is no logarithm at the minimal marked rate. |
| q-unmarked-two-scale-minimal-rate | unmarked expansion rate $\gamma$ | ≥ | $1/6$ | The minimal rate has no logarithm and subsequent rates have the stated gap. |
| q-irreducible-diameter-tail | irreducible bridge diameter tail | ≤ | $C r^{-3/4}$ | The diameter threshold is in the stated range. |
| q-growth-continuation-lower-mass | mass of length-controlled exact-wall continuations | ≥ | $c_\gamma S^{-1/4}(S/r)^{-C_{1,\gamma}}$ | The transverse error parameter is fixed before initial and target scales vary. |

          "exact_quote": "For each pairing pattern $p$ and seam sign $\\sigma\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$, the normalized physical vacuum has the form $$(V^\\sigma_N)_p(Z;t)\n    = \\frac{\\prod_j z_j^{o_j/2}}{A_\\delta(Z)}\\,\\psi_{p}(Z),\n \\qquad \\deg_{z_j}\\psi_p\\le\\delta(N-1)-o_j ,\n \\qquad\n \\delta=\\begin{cases}1&\\sigma=1,\\\\2&\\sigma=-1,\\end{cases}$$ independent of the row parameter. Here $o_j=1$ for an occupied port of pattern $p$, 0 otherwise. At adjacent high-then-low pairs of rapidities $q^a l,l$ the vector is $P V^\\sigma_{N-1}$ (fused rapidity $q l$) for $a=2$ and $C V^\\sigma_{N-2}$ for $a=3$, if the seam is not between the two. Branches at a specialization use $(q^a l)^{1/2}=q^{a/2}l^{1/2}$; thus this prescribes values of the branch-independent polynomials $\\psi$. The same parity convention holds under all transports. At a variable 0 or infinity the normalized vector $V$ with that port vacant goes to the vector with position dropped, and occupied components vanish there. There is covariance under nearest rapidity exchanges (by $B$, away from the seam) and cyclic rotations (moving the seam with the conjugating sign above), thus also rules across the seam by first rotating.",

There are also direct wall rules:*\n\n- *At a first parameter $p^{-1}$, insert a vacancy on either smaller vector, and in the odd vector also insert a defect there from the smaller even vector, summing; call this $K_L(E,O)$.*\n\n- *At a last parameter $p$, just insert a vacancy on each ($K_R$).*\n\n*Even vectors drop a vacant site at $z=0,\\infty$ (occupied components go to zero). Odd vectors also satisfy the following edge refinements at the first or last site $e$. With it occupied and paired, $V$ has vanishing leading term both at $z_e=0,\\infty$ on the scale $z_e^{\\mp1/2}$, respectively. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-periodic-vacuum-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-vacuum-polynomials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-vacuum-polynomials): Verify swap, triangle, and cap relations; prescribe compatible pair-hyperplane values; construct polynomial coordinate charts; prove row invariance and identify the result by physical vacuum uniqueness. -> Polynomial numerators, degree bounds, pair reductions, and extreme-variable rules for periodic vacua.
- **step-periodic-physical-gluing** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-periodic) / [proof](#L:step3): Glue reflected vacua with positive or mixed seam twists, derive marked-row evaluation constraints, and identify the rational observables by reductions, degrees, and endpoint values. -> Exact marked-loop, winding-loop, and color-sum identities.
- **step-scalar-trace-transformation** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-scalar-channel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-scalar-channel): Establish finite coefficient support, conjugate color shifts into the twist, evaluate oscillator contractions, and transform the fixed-axis Gaussian sum. Compare Fourier kernels and collision self factors. -> Scalar channel identity with explicit self, cross, kinetic, and oscillator factors.
- **step-long-circle-asymptotics** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-long-circle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-long-circle): Recover the finite sum by constant coefficient extraction. Use covariance coercivity and bin stability to localize coupled roots, expand source interactions, and convolve the finitely many cheap free gaps with summable coefficient extensions. -> Compatible rational-power and logarithmic expansions, allowing a possibly vanishing leading constant.
- **step-positive-amplitude-calibration** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-positive-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-positive-normalization): Express confluent polynomials through positive monic norms. Compare with explicit Fourier weights, use imaginary polynomial evaluation sums to prevent excessive determinant decay, and take positive roots of compatible expansions. -> Nonzero positive homogeneous amplitudes and expansions stable under fixed finite differences.
- **step-laplace-first-correction** [claim](#stmt-l-lem-polynomial-laplace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-lem-polynomial-laplace): Extract Jacobi coefficients from polynomial ratios and compare their flow to the explicit unperturbed propagator. A positive Duhamel representation and inverse-moment bounds give the Laplace correction. -> Controlled Laplace asymptotics needed for winding and strip cancellations.
- **step-pressure-root-inverse** [claim](#stmt-l-lem-pressure-root-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-lem-pressure-root-continuation): Locate roots through cumulative discrepancy, bound kernel variation, prove weighted diagonal dominance separately in the bulk and at both ends, and apply a uniform analytic contraction. -> Uniform weighted inverse and analytic continuation of root positions.
- **step-pressure-quadrature-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-l-lem-pressure-quadrature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-lem-pressure-quadrature): Represent discrepancy by a mean-zero sawtooth in the increasing phase region, integrate by parts using periodic primitives, and bound the exterior directly by test decay. -> Uniform cancellation estimate for pressure variation.
- **step-cylinder-pressure-to-height-tails** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-cylinder-height-tail) / [proof](#pm-h-43): Use root-product quadrature to bound continued pressure. Connected-cluster signs convert pressure bounds into positive coefficients; winding overlap counts yield height moments, and a local join transfers the tail to contractible polygons. -> Exponential winding height moment and contractible height tail.
- **step-open-strip-vector-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials): Construct compatible pair and wall data, interpolate coordinate charts, prove paired-edge vanishing and defect leading coefficients, then establish exchange covariance and both row equations before physical identification. -> Polynomial half-strip vectors with wall, pair, and edge rules.
- **step-strip-crossing-determinant** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-strip-mass) / [proof](#L:step9): Glue the half-strip vectors to obtain the crossing determinant. Reduce square-point confluence to residual Hermite evaluation determinants in a positive Gram space, retain the first nonvanishing column cancellation, and transfer the estimate by positive column comparison. -> Matching crossing-mass bounds at every honeycomb strip height.
- **step-doubled-root-stability** [claim](#stmt-l-lem-doubled-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-lem-doubled-stability): Check positive definiteness of polynomial coefficient matrices in common and difference sectors. Borrow kinetic covariance, subtract a spatially positive auxiliary kernel, and smooth the residual to obtain a bin-square penalty. -> Stable doubled trace with a retained kinetic margin and bend control.
- **step-two-port-polynomial-identity** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity): Compare denominator-cleared numerators by pair reductions, removals, and mark slides. Control the residual polynomial at nearly balanced family sizes and eliminate the remaining ambiguity by paired specializations. -> Exact single-loop observable through the named ports.
- **step-uniform-two-scale-integration** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-two-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-two-scale): Insert probe charges, remove bulk factors, and choose suppressive contours. Keep the scale-ratio edge separate from free propagation, Taylor expand sources with a fixed per-root base, convolve cheap gaps, and extend coefficients using the retained stability margins. -> Uniform two-scale expansions with accuracy-independent scale-ratio exponent and minimal-rate control.
- **step-enclosing-gas-to-chord-moments** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-box-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-box-moment): Calibrate the enclosing-loop cylinder gas, lift it to plane domains using height tails, and reverse flux through a punctured triangle. Sum the resulting visit bound over centers, then translate confined bridges across a common larger box. -> Finite-domain chord first-length bound and confined bridge first-length bound.
- **step-length-controlled-recoverable-growth** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-length-controlled-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-length-controlled-growth): Produce clipped exits with endpoint clearance, prune excessive lengths by the chord moment, and extract first qualifying cuts. Continue through geometrically growing confined bridge increments with terminal signs and an exact final target. -> Positive prefix mass and exact-wall continuations with controlled geometry, length, and recovery.
- **step-prescribed-gap-caps** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-caps): Use recoverable growth and rotated boundary flux to obtain a substantial gap on each scale. Add a thin boundary bump, compare slant exits, and attach fixed exterior extensions to transfer the mass to every smaller prescribed gap. -> Diameter-confined cap lower bounds and unrestricted gap comparison.
- **step-critical-irreducible-renewal** [claim](#stmt-l-prop-irreducible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-irreducible): Factor strict bridges at all single-crossing levels. Invert the nonnegative generating-series identity, pass monotonically to criticality, and bound diameter tails using confined concatenations. -> Normalized irreducible law, height-transform comparison, diameter tail, and independent infinite concatenation.
- **step-logarithmic-window-sewing** [claim](#stmt-l-thm-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-irreducible-2): Calibrate the oblique marked observable and count polygon vertex pairs, treating narrow polygons by cylinder tails. Sew caps to obtain geometric length moments and aspect suppression, prune exceptional path classes, attach disjoint length-controlled connectors, and control recovery by factorial double-cut moments before summing heights. -> The height-averaged critical bridge mass in the explicit logarithmic length window.
- **step-finite-estimate-assembly** [claim](#stmt-l-thm-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-l-prop-irreducible-3): Assemble the strip crossing determinant bound, prescribed-gap caps and matching arch upper bound, transverse tightness, confined chord moment, aspect tail, and polygon second moment. -> The complete finite boundary and polygon estimate collection.

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-long-circle",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\phi\\in\\{-7\\pi/8,-\\pi/8\\}$. Let $S_{\\rm raw}(N,\\phi)$ denote the confluent raw sum just considered. With $$E(y)=y^2/(2H_b),\\quad f_0=-\\phi/(2\\pi),\\quad\n {\\cal R}=d_{M_b}(a)/d_O(a)=(2k/3)^2/\\sqrt{2k}$$ (the last by the row formulas), define $$F_N(\\phi)=\n \\frac{S_{\\rm raw}(N,\\phi)}\n {|w|^N{\\cal R}^{N^2}N^{7/16-12E(f_0)}}.$$ There is a locally finite set $\\Gamma_\\phi\\subset\\mathbb Q_{\\ge0}$, containing $0$, and fixed polynomials $P_{\\phi,\\gamma}$ such that, for every desired accuracy $J>0$, one can choose $A_J>J$ with $$F_N(\\phi)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_\\phi\\\\ \\gamma\\le A_J}}\n N^{-\\gamma}P_{\\phi,\\gamma}(\\log N)+O_{\\phi,J}(N^{-J})$$ for every sufficiently large integer $N$. The coefficient polynomials are the same for all truncations and all integers $N$; no subsequence or parity choice is made. The polynomial at $\\gamma=0$ is a constant, which may at this stage vanish. For $\\phi=-\\pi/8$, every positive rate is at least $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The result controls infinite root expansions and asymptotic remainders with existential thresholds. A finite evaluation of the color sum does not certify these uniform asymptotics.",
      "step_id": "step-long-circle-asymptotics"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-43",
          "exact_quote": "On a finite long strip of this cylinder with vacant boundary input and output the partition function is a gas of pairwise disjoint winding polygons. Its log per row coefficients tend to $[a^p]L_n$ by simplicity/dominance locally at zero fugacity.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The tail argument passes through analytic continuation, an infinite-volume pressure limit, and summation of all height moments; it is not a finite enumeration certificate.",
      "step_id": "step-cylinder-pressure-to-height-tails"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-l-prop-irreducible",
          "exact_quote": "Monotone convergence as $t\\downarrow0$ shows $\\sum i_j=1$ and proves the height claim.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-irreducible",
          "exact_quote": "Independent samples may be concatenated to form an infinite self-avoiding port path.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "Critical normalization is obtained by monotone convergence of an infinite generating series, and the path construction uses infinitely many independent bridge pieces.",
      "step_id": "step-critical-irreducible-renewal"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-thm-window",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $c,C>0$ and $h_0$ such that, for every real $h\\ge h_0$, with $$R_h=h(\\log h)^{1/64},\\qquad\n \\ell_-(h)=h^{4/3}(\\log h)^{-1/8},\\qquad\n \\ell_+(h)=h^{4/3}(\\log h)^{1/2},$$ we have $$\\sum_{1\\le H\\le 2R_h}\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\omega\\in\\mathcal B_H\\\\\n       \\ell_-(h)\\le|\\omega|\\le2\\ell_+(h)}}\\mu(\\omega)\n \\ge c h^{3/4}(\\log h)^{-C}.$$ The sum over $H$ is over integer lattice heights.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The main theorem asserts sufficiently large-scale mass bounds with unspecified constants. The positive sewing argument provides recoverable families, but no finite decision procedure for the full asymptotic assertion.",
      "step_id": "step-logarithmic-window-sewing"
    }
  ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-prop-vacuum-polynomials",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-vacuum-polynomials",
          "exact_quote": "For each pairing pattern $p$ and seam sign $\\sigma\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$, the normalized physical vacuum has the form $$(V^\\sigma_N)_p(Z;t)\n    = \\frac{\\prod_j z_j^{o_j/2}}{A_\\delta(Z)}\\,\\psi_{p}(Z),\n \\qquad \\deg_{z_j}\\psi_p\\le\\delta(N-1)-o_j ,\n \\qquad\n \\delta=\\begin{cases}1&\\sigma=1,\\\\2&\\sigma=-1,\\end{cases}$$ independent of the row parameter. Here $o_j=1$ for an occupied port of pattern $p$, 0 otherwise. At adjacent high-then-low pairs of rapidities $q^a l,l$ the vector is $P V^\\sigma_{N-1}$ (fused rapidity $q l$) for $a=2$ and $C V^\\sigma_{N-2}$ for $a=3$, if the seam is not between the two. Branches at a specialization use $(q^a l)^{1/2}=q^{a/2}l^{1/2}$; thus this prescribes values of the branch-independent polynomials $\\psi$. The same parity convention holds under all transports. At a variable 0 or infinity the normalized vector $V$ with that port vacant goes to the vector with position dropped, and occupied components vanish there. There is covariance under nearest rapidity exchanges (by $B$, away from the seam) and cyclic rotations (moving the seam with the conjugating sign above), thus also rules across the seam by first rotating.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-periodic-vacuum-interpolation",
      "inputs": [
        "Boundary flux bound and convergent half-cylinder vacua",
        "Periodic Pfaffian and determinant reduction rules",
        "Noncrossing pairing states and seam covariance"
      ],
      "method": "Verify swap, triangle, and cap relations; prescribe compatible pair-hyperplane values; construct polynomial coordinate charts; prove row invariance and identify the result by physical vacuum uniqueness.",
      "output": "Polynomial numerators, degree bounds, pair reductions, and extreme-variable rules for periodic vacua.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-l-prop-vacuum-polynomials"

          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-periodic",
          "exact_quote": "For the above periodic model, the marked-loop mass, winding-loop mass modulo row translations, and color sum satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n E_+(Z;t)&=1+\\kappa\\left(\\frac{Q(Z,t)Q(Z,q^3t)}\n {Q(Z)^2\\prod_{z\\in Z}d(z,t)}-1\\right),\\label{L:eq:one-port}\\\\\n Y(Z)J(Z;t)&=\\frac{(-1)^{N+1}}{\\sqrt2}\n [p_Z^+(t)p_Z^-(q^3t)+p_Z^-(t)p_Z^+(q^3t)],\\label{L:eq:winding}\\\\\n O_Z(1)&=1,\\qquad O_Z(\\kappa)=Y(Z),\\qquad\n O_Z(\\kappa^2)=Y(Z)^2.\\label{L:eq:color-values}\n\\end{aligned}$$ These identities hold initially at physical parameters and their convergence neighborhoods, and elsewhere by rational continuation. Replacing $q$ by $q^{-1}$ in the color factors has the same effect as exchanging $+$ and $-$, including $b_+,b_-$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-periodic-physical-gluing",
      "inputs": [
        "Polynomial half-cylinder vectors",

        "Color residue cancellations"
      ],
      "method": "Glue reflected vacua with positive or mixed seam twists, derive marked-row evaluation constraints, and identify the rational observables by reductions, degrees, and endpoint values.",
      "output": "Exact marked-loop, winding-loop, and color-sum identities.",
      "proof_anchor": "L:step3"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-prop-scalar-channel",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-scalar-channel",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $L>0$, ordered angular layers with the prescribed common-layer boundary values, and a finite ordered list of fields with charges $\\beta_i$ as above. Use the indicated lifts of $O$ and $M_b$ and the cocycle convention of the fields $V_{\\beta_i}$. For $U=M_b$, impose the neutrality condition $\\sum_i h\\beta_i=0$; without it the scalar trace contribution is zero. Let $\\mathcal S_i$ be the displayed self multiplier, $\\mathcal C_{ij}$ the exponential of the displayed short-range cross logarithm for $x_i<x_j$, and $\\mathcal K_f$ the displayed kinetic factor. The angular trace contribution for this list is $$\\label{L:eq:scalar-channel}\n \\mathcal T_U(\\boldsymbol\\beta,\\boldsymbol u)\n =Z_U a_U\\sum_{f\\in\\mathcal F_U}\n   e^{\\pi i\\Phi}\\mathcal K_f\n   \\prod_i\\mathcal S_i\\prod_{x_i<x_j}\\mathcal C_{ij},\n \\qquad\n a_{M_b}=\\sqrt{\\frac{L}{2\\pi H_b}},\\quad a_O=1.$$ Here $\\mathcal F_{M_b}=-\\phi/(2\\pi)+\\mathbb Z$. For $O$ the sum consists of one term, $\\mathcal K_f=1$, and all fixed-axis terms in $\\Phi$ are omitted. The identity first holds with the separated representatives and prescribed boundary values above; Section <a href=\"#L:step6\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"L:step6\">3.4</a> supplies the convergence and continuation required for the full root expansion.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }

          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\phi\\in\\{-7\\pi/8,-\\pi/8\\}$. Let $S_{\\rm raw}(N,\\phi)$ denote the confluent raw sum just considered. With $$E(y)=y^2/(2H_b),\\quad f_0=-\\phi/(2\\pi),\\quad\n {\\cal R}=d_{M_b}(a)/d_O(a)=(2k/3)^2/\\sqrt{2k}$$ (the last by the row formulas), define $$F_N(\\phi)=\n \\frac{S_{\\rm raw}(N,\\phi)}\n {|w|^N{\\cal R}^{N^2}N^{7/16-12E(f_0)}}.$$ There is a locally finite set $\\Gamma_\\phi\\subset\\mathbb Q_{\\ge0}$, containing $0$, and fixed polynomials $P_{\\phi,\\gamma}$ such that, for every desired accuracy $J>0$, one can choose $A_J>J$ with $$F_N(\\phi)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_\\phi\\\\ \\gamma\\le A_J}}\n N^{-\\gamma}P_{\\phi,\\gamma}(\\log N)+O_{\\phi,J}(N^{-J})$$ for every sufficiently large integer $N$. The coefficient polynomials are the same for all truncations and all integers $N$; no subsequence or parity choice is made. The polynomial at $\\gamma=0$ is a constant, which may at this stage vanish. For $\\phi=-\\pi/8$, every positive rate is at least $1$.",

      "id": "step-long-circle-asymptotics",
      "inputs": [
        "Scalar channel transformation",
        "Root Fourier coercivity and bin-square stability",

      "method": "Recover the finite sum by constant coefficient extraction. Use covariance coercivity and bin stability to localize coupled roots, expand source interactions, and convolve the finitely many cheap free gaps with summable coefficient extensions.",
      "output": "Compatible rational-power and logarithmic expansions, allowing a possibly vanishing leading constant.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-l-prop-long-circle"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-prop-positive-normalization",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-positive-normalization",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_Q,c_R>0$ such that $$Q_n\\sim c_Q B_Q(n)n^{5/48},\\qquad\n R_n\\sim c_R B_R(n)n^{-7/24}.$$ After these leading factors are removed, both sequences have expansions in terms $n^{-\\gamma}$ with rational $\\gamma\\ge0$ and polynomial coefficients in $\\log n$, to arbitrary inverse-power accuracy. The positive constant leading terms and all coefficient polynomials are common to all integer sizes. Any fixed number of finite differences may be taken by first choosing the expansion to sufficiently high accuracy.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"

      "id": "step-positive-amplitude-calibration",
      "inputs": [

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-lem-polynomial-laplace",

          "anchor": "stmt-l-lem-polynomial-laplace",
          "exact_quote": "The positive Jacobi off-diagonals for $w_b$ satisfy $$\\sqrt{h_n/h_{n-1}}= n(2c)^{-1} s_n,\\qquad s_n^2=1+\\frac{d_0}{n^2}+O(n^{-2-\\epsilon})$$ for some real $d_0$ and some $\\epsilon>0$; the on-diagonals vanish by symmetry. Put $S_n=\\prod_{j=1}^n s_j$; then $h_0 S_\\infty^2=k/(2c\\sqrt2)$. For $0<\\tau<\\pi/2$, the ratio below is $1+O(n^{-1})$, locally uniformly in $\\tau$, and has the pointwise asymptotic $$\\label{L:eq:laplace-correction}\n \\frac{f_n(2c\\tau)}{\\sqrt{h_0}\\, S_n \\sec\\tau\\tan^n\\tau}\n       =1+\\frac{d(\\tau)}{n}+o(n^{-1}).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-cylinder-height-tail",

      "method": "Use root-product quadrature to bound continued pressure. Connected-cluster signs convert pressure bounds into positive coefficients; winding overlap counts yield height moments, and a local join transfers the tail to contractible polygons.",
      "output": "Exponential winding height moment and contractible height tail.",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-43"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-strip-polynomials",
          "exact_quote": "For either vector $V=E,O$, with $\\sigma=-1,+1$ respectively, let $o_j$ denote the occupations of the output pattern. Then $$\\begin{gathered}\n D_n=Q_o\\prod_j z_j^{n-1},\\qquad\n \\psi= D_n \\left(\\prod_j z_j^{\\sigma o_j/2}\\right)V,\\\\\n \\psi\\text{ is polynomial with }\\deg_{z_j}\\psi\\le m_j=2(n-1)+\\sigma o_j.\n\\end{gathered}$$ At ordered neighboring high-low parameters $q^r l,l$ for $r=2,3$, the vector value uses respectively $P V$ (at fused parameter $q l$) and $C V$ (dropping the pair). There are also direct wall rules:*\n\n- *At a first parameter $p^{-1}$, insert a vacancy on either smaller vector, and in the odd vector also insert a defect there from the smaller even vector, summing; call this $K_L(E,O)$.*\n\n- *At a last parameter $p$, just insert a vacancy on each ($K_R$).*\n\n*Even vectors drop a vacant site at $z=0,\\infty$ (occupied components go to zero). Odd vectors also satisfy the following edge refinements at the first or last site $e$. With it occupied and paired, $V$ has vanishing leading term both at $z_e=0,\\infty$ on the scale $z_e^{\\mp1/2}$, respectively. With it the defect, $$\\lim_{z_e\\to\\infty} V/\\sqrt{z_e}=c_* E_{\\rm dropped},\\qquad\n c_*=[z^{1/2}]g_z$$ where the bracket on the right denotes a Laurent coefficient. All parameter rules concern generic other values.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "There are constants $0<c<C<\\infty$ such that, for every integer $H\\ge1$, $$cH^{-1/4}\\le B_H\\le CH^{-1/4}.$$ The bound holds in each of the three lattice-side directions.",

      "method": "Glue the half-strip vectors to obtain the crossing determinant. Reduce square-point confluence to residual Hermite evaluation determinants in a positive Gram space, retain the first nonvanishing column cancellation, and transfer the estimate by positive column comparison.",
      "output": "Matching crossing-mass bounds at every honeycomb strip height.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-lem-doubled-stability",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-scalar-trace-transformation"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-lem-doubled-stability",
          "exact_quote": "For the ten root species and angular layers in <a href=\"#L:eq3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"L:eq3\">[L:eq3]</a>, one can choose $d_f<1$ sufficiently close to $1$ and constants $0<\\eta<1$ and $c,C>0$ so that the following holds. If the total root $h$-charge is zero, $K$ roots lie on a real circle of sufficiently large period $L$, and $K_I$ counts them in successive bins of lengths between $1/2$ and $2$, then, for every allowed axis index $f$, $$|\\mathscr W_{\\rm root}|\\,\n \\exp\\!\\left[-(1-\\eta)\\sum_{\\rm gaps}\n       \\Delta x\\,E\\Bigl(f+\\sum_{\\rm before}hr_i\\Bigr)\\right]\n \\le \\exp\\!\\left(CK-c\\sum_I K_I^2\\right),\n \\qquad E(y)=\\frac{y^2}{2H}.$$ The constants are uniform for the common small-slope bends used in the change of channel, with real gap lengths in the kinetic modulus. They are locally uniform at bounded positive real periods as well, with bin lengths bounded above and below by positive constants chosen for the compact period interval.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-doubled-root-stability",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Check positive definiteness of polynomial coefficient matrices in common and difference sectors. Borrow kinetic covariance, subtract a spatially positive auxiliary kernel, and smooth the residual to obtain a bin-square penalty.",
      "output": "Stable doubled trace with a retained kinetic margin and bend control.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-periodic-vacuum-interpolation",
        "step-periodic-physical-gluing"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity",
          "exact_quote": "$$Q(a,\\mathcal X,b,\\mathcal Y)^2 C_{ab} =\n \\rho a b \\prod_{i<j}D_{\\sigma_i\\sigma_j}\n \\sum_{\\sum \\sigma_i t_{c_i}=0}\n \\prod_{i<j}P^{\\sigma_i\\sigma_j}_{c_i c_j}(z_j/z_i)\n \\prod_i w_{c_i}^{\\sigma_i} f_{c_i}(a,z_i) f_{c_i}(b,z_i)\\label{L:eq7}$$ where $f_c(p,z)=(p-q^r z)(p-q^{r'}z)$ for $(r,r')=(5,6),(3,5),(2,3)$ respectively.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.6"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-two-port-polynomial-identity",
      "inputs": [
        "Positive periodic vacua",
        "Divided-slice neutral color identity",
        "Mark polynomial factors"
      ],
      "method": "Compare denominator-cleared numerators by pair reductions, removals, and mark slides. Control the residual polynomial at nearly balanced family sizes and eliminate the remaining ambiguity by paired specializations.",
      "output": "Exact single-loop observable through the named ports.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-l-prop-two-port-identity"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-l-prop-two-scale",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-doubled-root-stability",
        "step-two-port-polynomial-identity"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-l-prop-two-scale",
          "exact_quote": "For an admissible integer array of either of the two kinds specified above, put $$G_Z^{(p_*)}=\\frac{F_Z^{(p_*)}}{{\\cal D}(Z)^2(K_0s_1)^{3/8}}.$$ For each $J>0$ and each of the fixed composition neighborhoods, there is a threshold $s_J$ independent of $K_0\\ge1$ such that, on every fixed phase class and every admissible integer array with $s_1\\ge s_J$, this quantity has a finite approximation $$G_Z^{(p_*)}=\n \\sum_\\gamma s_1^{-\\gamma}P_\\gamma(\\log s_1;K_0,\\text{parameters})\n       +O_J(K_0^{C_2}s_1^{-J}).\\label{L:eq9}$$ The formula is asserted at admissible integer arrays. Its coefficient functions extend holomorphically to the composition neighborhoods above and are bounded by $C_JK_0^{C_2}$. One exponent $C_2$ works for every accuracy $J$; the finite rate and degree lists may depend on $J$ but not on $K_0$. In the marked case $\\gamma\\ge3/2$, with no logarithm at equality. In the unmarked case $\\gamma\\ge1/6$, with no logarithm at equality, and all other rates are at least $1/2$ higher.",

The cuts separating all stages are recovered by the same first-cut rule.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-height-averaged-window**: The critical bridge mass has a logarithmic length window after summing over the stated integer heights; the conclusion retains its height averaging.
- **invariant-confined-first-moment**: The bridge mass lower bound and length-weighted mass upper bound hold together under the stated fixed transverse confinement. Either weak terminal displacement sign is permitted in the mass lower bound.
- **invariant-physical-versus-rational-identities**: The cylinder identities describe convergent physical masses initially at physical parameters and their convergence neighborhoods; their extension elsewhere is by rational continuation.
- **invariant-scalar-neutrality**: The transformed scalar trace requires the stated neutrality condition; without neutrality its contribution vanishes.
- **invariant-positive-leading-amplitudes**: The homogeneous polynomial normalizations have strictly positive leading constants common to all integer sizes. Finite differences require choosing the expansion accuracy sufficiently high first.
- **invariant-recoverable-growth**: The first qualifying cut depends on prefix conditions alone and remains recoverable after extension strictly beyond that cut. This recovery is required when multiplying masses of concatenated families.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-fixed-height-or-exact-length**: Treat the window theorem as a lower bound at a prescribed height, endpoint, or exact length. -> The theorem sums over heights and a length interval. It does not select the prescribed path parameters.
- **anti-endpoint-length-conventions**: Substitute edge length for center length when gluing port paths. -> Changing the endpoint convention invalidates direct use of the stated weights and length thresholds.
- **anti-unconfined-first-moment**: Apply the confined bridge first-moment estimate to paths with a growing transverse cutoff without accounting for additional losses. -> The fixed-confinement estimate does not supply the later height-averaged bounds with larger cutoffs.
- **anti-rational-continuation-as-positive-mass**: Interpret every continued rational cylinder value as a convergent positive loop mass. -> Rational continuation supplies an algebraic value outside the initially established physical convergence neighborhoods.
- **anti-omit-neutrality**: Evaluate the transformed scalar trace with an arbitrary total fixed-axis charge. -> A nonneutral field list has zero scalar trace contribution in the transformed channel.
- **anti-asymptotic-exponent-without-amplitude**: Infer a positive physical mass asymptotic from a formal exponent before checking its leading coefficient. -> The long-circle expansion initially permits the leading constant to vanish.
- **anti-renewal-law-as-window-proof**: Treat the irreducible bridge probability law as a substitute for the window proof. -> The paper presents the renewal law as a separate consequence; the window requires finite weighted counts and recoverable sewing.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The bridge-length window is an asymptotic lower bound over unbounded path families with existential constants and a sufficiently large height threshold. Its finite transfer identities are proof ingredients rather than a finite decision procedure for that lower bound. The introduction explicitly states: "The averaging in height is part of the conclusion." It also states: "Passing from such a window to one prescribed length requires a further local renewal argument." Finite enumeration cannot certify the full quantified theorem, its infinite path sums, or its unspecified threshold.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bridge window bounds a critical lattice-path measure; it supplies no software implementation or executable interface. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The irreducible-bridge law permits independent concatenation, but the paper supplies no finite sampling procedure for that distribution. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scalar change of channel evaluates analytical trace contributions under prescribed charge and boundary conventions; no operational analysis tool is constructed. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For a prescribed finite collection of cells, construct the weighted diagram sum by composing the listed local matchings, enforcing agreement at shared ports and multiplying compatible weights. This is a finite combinatorial construction; identifying its limits with infinite-cylinder observables requires the separate convergence arguments. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A local cell state has a concrete representation as a noncrossing matching of a subset of its named ports. Composition requires both occupancy and connectivity, so the matching itself provides a suitable finite diagram representation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polynomial half-strip representations establish degree and boundary rules, but do not specify a hardware mapping, resource requirement, or accelerator construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polynomial vacuum vectors describe physical pairing states and seam conventions; they provide no cryptographic primitive or security assumption. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite chord estimate controls length-weighted critical path mass in convex lattice domains. It does not construct a learning model, estimator, or training procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Recoverable half-plane growth constructs weighted families with geometric and length constraints, but supplies no objective-minimising path algorithm or optimality certificate. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cylinder-height tails concern winding and contractible lattice polygons counted modulo row translations. They establish no communication protocol, replication rule, or distributed agreement property. |
| other (Statistical mechanics of lattice loop models) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs a local rhombic loop model whose specified honeycomb specialisation assigns the critical weight per visited center. A finite model can therefore be assembled from the stated cell states and weights while preserving the physical endpoint convention. |
